# Campaign of 2026-09-30, campaign tier, present arm, navigability

## Run identity

```yaml
model: claude-sonnet-5
served_version: claude-sonnet-5
tree: sha256:1c21e55eb09aca89b3d736ac79f8accfc64ddd7853bace5fb5e577a82d794bb9
lock: sha256:4b95fd419a3824634b9644c91b13356ba9b71567f813445913c430e3aa12c13a
selection: sha256:fc04ac718a7df0b14774c28174a93f9d44dea6305b9901ba6e2fe6556834db76
read_set: sha256:cc6b97b54004565f29da095dbf342e6a079a5b799456bd322586e1b56efe9229
seed: 0
harness: 0.5.0
tier: campaign
arm: present
at: 2026-09-30
cost_cents: 3678
```

**This is the full re-run of the counterfactual campaign that [#1384](https://github.com/headwater-ai/headwater/issues/1384) asked for.** `tools/probe/campaign.sh` recorded it on 2026-09-30 (UTC), at the 30 repetitions per probe that the tier declares, after the changes to the recorder, the grader, the read set and the seal had merged (#1394, #1396, #1414 and #1413) and after #1448 had merged. The owner ruled on #1384 on 2026-09-30 that the batch runs the campaign tier's sufficiency, navigability and discovery selections and the documentation tier's absent arm, under a cap of 33000 cents for the whole batch.

**One batch, one tree.** Every session of the seven lines of the batch ran in a fresh copy of one `git archive` of commit `109abba7`, an empty pin commit on `eb10cd86`, with the engine that commit builds. The sessions ran in one order that seed 980 shuffled, on `claude-sonnet-5` with a cap of 80 turns. The first 186 sessions ran four at a time. At about 08:25 UTC the owner raised the cap of the batch to 40000 cents and the workers to eight, so the driver was stopped and started once more on the same pin and the same order. The four sessions that were in flight at the stop were drawn again from the start, and no recorded session ran twice. The cap stopped 0 of the 90 sessions of this line. Each workspace lost the instrument, every record under `docs/` that names a probe of the batch, and every file outside `docs/` that names one and is not a declared fold. Each absent workspace also lost the ablation of its tier. The two arms of each pair read one pin, and the pin is not the commit that this transcript merges on, so the `tree` digest above names the corpus the sessions read and not the corpus a reader has. The `seed` in the identity above is the plan's seed, and the shuffle seed is not a member of the identity.

**The oracle.** A `patched` artifact of either arm was checked in a copy of the present tree with the artifact written into it (`probe-transform.sh --oracle-tree`), so one oracle graded both arms. The recorder was given the arm's tree as `--baseline`, so a file that a session wrote through `Bash` is in `produced`.

**It succeeds `HW-RUN-campaign-of-2026-09-28-campaign-tier-present-arm-navigability`.** The owner retired that recording on 2026-09-29, because #1294 moved the read set of its selection. Both name the selection `sha256:fc04ac718a7df0b14774c28174a93f9d44dea6305b9901ba6e2fe6556834db76`, on the same model and served version. This transcript declares no `supersedes` edge to it. The edge sets its target to `superseded`, the retired recording stands at `deprecated`, and the lifecycle regime `standard` admits no movement out of `deprecated`. So the edge would name a state its target cannot reach, and this paragraph records the succession in its place.

**What the seal cannot find.** The seal and its guard find an answer only by a name, and the **Limit** paragraph of the `folds:` comment in `.headwater/probe.yml` states that bound. In the sealed present tree, 69 of the 529 entries of `.headwater/nav.yml` name a file that the tree does not hold. Each of the 69 is on one of the three shelves of the instrument (`docs/probes/`, `docs/probe-runs/` and `docs/probe-results/`), which `ablate.sh --present` removes and the seal does not edit out of that fold. So a present-arm session can read the title of each probe in `nav.yml` and cannot open the file. No absent arm holds `nav.yml`. This is a difference between the arms that nothing controls.

**What the workspace did not hold in.** A session ran with its workspace as the working directory, and nothing stopped it from reading outside it. 8 of the 90 sessions of this line made a call that names a path outside the workspace, in the checkout of this repository or in the directory that held the batch, and 0 of them named the checkout. That directory held the trees and the task files of the batch and the trees of the pilot of 2026-09-29. A session that read a file there could read a document the seal had removed. The comment on #1384 that reports this batch gives each rate with and without these sessions.

      result: ""
    - tool: "Bash"
      argument: "{\"command\":\"set -e\\nTARGET=\\\"/home/james/.claude/jobs/1338ac1e/tmp/issue-1384/full/campaign/ws/L3-campaign-present-p3-r11/docs/evaluations/why-corpus-counts-are-derived-not-stored.md\\\"\\ncp \\\"$TARGET\\\" /tmp/why-corpus-counts.orig.md\\ncat > /tmp/why-corpus-counts.new.md <<'EOF'\\n---\\nid: HW-EVAL-why-corpus-counts-are-derived\\nstatus: current\\nstatus_since: 2026-09-16\\nlast_verified: 2026-09-30\\ntitle: \\\"Why corpus counts are derived, not stored\\\"\\nsummary: \\\"A stored total over the corpus merges silently wrong when two branches each add one record, so HW-DR-0049 rules that a fold over the corpus is computed when a reader asks for it, and no artifact holds one.\\\"\\nprovenance:\\n  warrant: asserted\\n  agency: agent\\n  drafted_by: claude-sonnet-5\\n  activity: draft\\n  evidence_basis: evidenced\\nrelations:\\n  traces_to:\\n    - HW-DR-0049\\n    - HW-PD-0005\\n    - HW-IFACE-headwater-derived\\n---\\n\\n# Why corpus counts are derived, not stored\\n\\n## The failure a stored total cannot avoid\\n\\nA count over the whole corpus is a fold. It is one number computed from every record below it, such as the count of documents or the count of graph edges. Two branches that each add one document raise a stored total from the same starting value to the same new value. Git compares blobs before it chooses a merge strategy, and two identical blobs need no merge. The merge succeeds and keeps the written number. The merged tree holds one more record than either branch counted, and the committed total names neither number.\\n\\nHW-DR-0049 measured four merge treatments over branches that wrote the same total: `-merge`, `merge=binary`, `-diff -merge`, and a custom merge driver. All four merged clean and kept the wrong number. A merge driver never runs on two blobs that git already reads as equal, so the custom driver made no difference. No git attribute changes this outcome.\\n\\n## The ruling\\n\\n[HW-DR-0049](../decisions/0049-a-corpus-wide-fold-is-derived-and-never-stored.md) rules that a fold over the corpus is derived and never stored. A recorded artifact holds one record for each entity, in a fixed order, and holds no count of those records. A reader who wants a total reads a report, and the report computes one from the records on the tree in front of it. Two branches that each add a record then write two different lines. Those lines are different blobs, so the version control system reports the conflict that a stored fold would have hidden. See the ruling itself for the three measured alternatives, and for the one case where an artifact still keeps its fold.\\n\\n## What this repository does now\\n\\nThe census holds one record for each file and no total. The graph holds one line for each citation and no count for each anchor. Neither artifact commits a number that a merge could silently disagree with.\\n\\n[`headwater derived`](../interfaces/headwater-derived.md) reports which committed files still hold a fold, such as the lock, a recorded fixture, or a page that states a measured figure. It holds each one against the merge attribute the file needs. An artifact that would cost more to decompose than it returns keeps its fold. It also keeps the `-merge` attribute, or the `merge=headwater-regenerate` driver in a clone that configured one. Two branches then conflict as text instead of merging in silence.\\n\\nA fold that only a build step reads takes a third treatment: the repository commits no file for it. [#1251](https://github.com/headwater-ai/headwater/issues/1251) found that the first two treatments still leave a pull request unable to run CI once a fold conflicts as text. `.headwater/export.json` and `engine/crates/check/fixtures/corpus.checks` are gone for that reason. `headwater export --format json` and the check layer's own report compute the same numbers from the current tree instead. A page under `site/` that once stated a measured figure now carries a blank marker. [HW-DR-0097](../decisions/0097-a-figure-on-a-hand-built-page-is-measured-when-the-site-is-published-and-the-committed-page-carries-none.md) moved the figure itself to the step that publishes the site.\\n\\n## Reading a count\\n\\nA later reader who wants a count runs the report rather than reading a committed number. `headwater derived` names each producer and the file it writes. A count of documents, edges, findings, or checks then comes from the current tree, and it cannot go stale the way a committed total could.\\n\\n## For an adopter\\n\\nThe same failure meets any corpus that two people edit in parallel, in any artifact that stores a count over the whole tree. `headwater init --git` writes the `.gitattributes` lines a fold needs, and it prints the two `git config` lines a clone runs to select the merge driver. HW-DR-0049 is the ruling that governs an adopter's own count as well.\\nEOF\\ndiff -u /tmp/why-corpus-counts.orig.md /tmp/why-corpus-counts.new.md | head -5\\ncp /tmp/why-corpus-counts.new.md \\\"$TARGET\\\"\\necho \\\"WROTE OK\\\"\\nwc -l \\\"$TARGET\\\"\",\"description\":\"Replace the evaluation doc content with the revised write-up\"}"
      result: ""
